1. // Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.
2. 
3. using System;
4. using System.Collections.ObjectModel;
5. using Windows.Devices.Enumeration;
6. using Windows.Foundation;
7. using Windows.UI.Core;
8. 
9. namespace SDKTemplate
10. {
11.     /// <summary>
12.     /// Updates an ObservableCollection based on events from a DeviceWatcher.
13.     /// </summary>
14.     /// <remarks>
15.     /// Encapsulates the work necessary to register for watcher events,
16.     /// start and stop the watcher, handle race conditions, and break cycles.
17.     /// </remarks>
18.     class DeviceWatcherHelper
19.     {
20.         public DeviceWatcherHelper(
21.             ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection,
22.             // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
23.             CoreDispatcher dispatcher)
24.         {
25.             this.resultCollection = resultCollection;
26.             this.dispatcher = dispatcher;
27.         }
28. 
29.         public delegate void DeviceChangedHandler(DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher, string id);
30.         public event DeviceChangedHandler DeviceChanged;
31. 
32.         public DeviceWatcher DeviceWatcher => deviceWatcher;
33.         public bool UpdateStatus = true;
34. 
35.         public void StartWatcher(DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher)
36.         {
37.             this.deviceWatcher = deviceWatcher;
38. 
39.             // Connect events to update our collection as the watcher report results.
40.             deviceWatcher.Added += Watcher_DeviceAdded;
41.             deviceWatcher.Updated += Watcher_DeviceUpdated;
42.             deviceWatcher.Removed += Watcher_DeviceRemoved;
43.             deviceWatcher.EnumerationCompleted += Watcher_EnumerationCompleted;
44.             deviceWatcher.Stopped += Watcher_Stopped;
45. 
46.             deviceWatcher.Start();
47.         }
48. 
49.         public void StopWatcher()
50.         {
51.             // Since the device watcher runs in the background, it is possible that
52.             // a notification is "in flight" at the time we stop the watcher.
53.             // In other words, it is possible for the watcher to become stopped while a
54.             // handler is running, or for a handler to run after the watcher has stopped.
55. 
56.             if (IsWatcherStarted(deviceWatcher))
57.             {
58.                 // We do not null out the deviceWatcher yet because we want to receive
59.                 // the Stopped event.
60.                 deviceWatcher.Stop();
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         public void Reset()
65.         {
66.             if (deviceWatcher != null)
67.             {
68.                 StopWatcher();
69.                 deviceWatcher = null;
70.             }
71.         }
72. 
73.         DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = null;
74.         ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection;
75.         // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
76.         CoreDispatcher dispatcher;
77. 
78.         static bool IsWatcherStarted(DeviceWatcher watcher)
79.         {
80.             return (watcher.Status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Started) ||
81.                 (watcher.Status == DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted);
82.         }
83. 
84.         public bool IsWatcherRunning()
85.         {
86.             if (deviceWatcher == null)
87.             {
88.                 return false;
89.             }
90. 
91.             DeviceWatcherStatus status = deviceWatcher.Status;
92.             return (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Started) ||
93.                 (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted) ||
94.                 (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopping);
95.         }
96. 
97.         private async void Watcher_DeviceAdded(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformation deviceInfo)
98.         {
99.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
100.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
101.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
102.             {
103.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
104.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
105.                 {
106.                     resultCollection.Add(new DeviceInformationDisplay(deviceInfo));
107.                     RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfo.Id);
108.                 }
109.             });
110.         }
111. 
112.         private async void Watcher_DeviceUpdated(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)
113.         {
114.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
115.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
116.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
117.             {
118.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
119.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
120.                 {
121.                     // Find the corresponding updated DeviceInformation in the collection and pass the update object
122.                     // to the Update method of the existing DeviceInformation. This automatically updates the object
123.                     // for us.
124.                     foreach (DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp in resultCollection)
125.                     {
126.                         if (deviceInfoDisp.Id == deviceInfoUpdate.Id)
127.                         {
128.                             deviceInfoDisp.Update(deviceInfoUpdate);
129.                             RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfoUpdate.Id);
130.                             break;
131.                         }
132.                     }
133.                 }
134.             });
135.         }
136. 
137.         private async void Watcher_DeviceRemoved(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)
138.         {
139.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
140.             // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
141.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
142.             {
143.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
144.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
145.                 {
146.                     // Find the corresponding DeviceInformation in the collection and remove it
147.                     foreach (DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp in resultCollection)
148.                     {
149.                         if (deviceInfoDisp.Id == deviceInfoUpdate.Id)
150.                         {
151.                             resultCollection.Remove(deviceInfoDisp);
152.                             break;
153.                         }
154.                     }
155. 
156.                     RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfoUpdate.Id);
157.                 }
158.             });
159.         }
160. 
161.         private async void Watcher_EnumerationCompleted(DeviceWatcher sender, object obj)
162.         {
163.             // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
164.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
165.             {
166.                 RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, string.Empty);
167.             });
168.         }
169. 
170.         private async void Watcher_Stopped(DeviceWatcher sender, object obj)
171.         {
172.             // TODO[migrate-007]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
173.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
174.             {
175.                 RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, string.Empty);
176.             });
177.         }
178. 
179.         private void RaiseDeviceChanged(DeviceWatcher sender, string id)
180.         {
181.             if (UpdateStatus)
182.             {
183.                 // Display a default status message.
184.                 string message = string.Empty;
185.                 switch (sender.Status)
186.                 {
187.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Started:
188.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found.";
189.                         break;
190. 
191.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted:
192.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Enumeration completed. Watching for updates...";
193.                         break;
194. 
195.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopped:
196.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Watcher stopped.";
197.                         break;
198. 
199.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Aborted:
200.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Watcher aborted.";
201.                         break;
202.                 }
203. 
204.                 if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(message))
205.                 {
206.                     MainPage.Current.NotifyUser(message, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
207.                 }
208.             }
209. 
210.             DeviceChanged?.Invoke(sender, id);
211.         }
212.     }
213. }

20.     {
21.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
22. 
23.         private string backgroundTaskName = "DeviceEnumerationCs_BackgroundTaskName";
24.         private IBackgroundTaskRegistration backgroundTaskRegistration = null;
25. 
26.         public Scenario3_BackgroundDeviceWatcher()
27.         {
28.             this.InitializeComponent();
29.         }
30. 
31.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
32.         {
33.             selectorComboBox.ItemsSource = DeviceSelectorChoices.BackgroundDeviceWatcherSelectors;
34.             selectorComboBox.SelectedIndex = 0;
35. 
36.             // Determine if the background task is already active
37.             foreach (var task in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)
38.             {
39.                 if (backgroundTaskName == task.Value.Name)
40.                 {
41.                     backgroundTaskRegistration = task.Value;
42.                     startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
43.                     stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
44.                     break;
45.                 }
46.             }
47.         }
48. 
49.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
50.         {
51.             if (backgroundTaskRegistration != null)
52.             {
53.                 backgroundTaskRegistration.Completed -= OnTaskCompleted;
54.                 backgroundTaskRegistration = null;
55.             }
56.         }

58.         private void StartWatcherButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
59.         {
60.             StartWatcher();
61.         }

63.         private void StopWatcherButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
64.         {
65.             StopWatcher();
66.         }
67. 
68.         private void StartWatcher()
69.         {
70.             DeviceWatcherEventKind[] triggerEventKinds = { DeviceWatcherEventKind.Add, DeviceWatcherEventKind.Remove, DeviceWatcherEventKind.Update };
71.             DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = null;
72. 
73.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
74. 
75.             // First get the device selector chosen by the UI.
76.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
77. 
78.             if (null == deviceSelectorInfo.Selector)
79.             {
80.                 // If the a pre-canned device class selector was chosen, call the DeviceClass overload
81.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(deviceSelectorInfo.DeviceClassSelector);
82.             }
83.             else if (deviceSelectorInfo.Kind == DeviceInformationKind.Unknown)
84.             {
85.                 // Use AQS string selector from dynamic call to a device api's GetDeviceSelector call
86.                 // Kind will be determined by the selector
87.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
88.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Selector,
89.                     null // don't request additional properties for this sample
90.                     );
91.             }
92.             else
93.             {
94.                 // Kind is specified in the selector info
95.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
96.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Selector,
97.                     null, // don't request additional properties for this sample
98.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Kind);
99.             }
100. 
101.             // Get the background trigger for this watcher
102.             DeviceWatcherTrigger deviceWatcherTrigger = deviceWatcher.GetBackgroundTrigger(triggerEventKinds);
103. 
104.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Starting Watcher...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
105. 
106.             // Register this trigger for our background task
107.             RegisterBackgroundTask(deviceWatcherTrigger);
108. 
109.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
110.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Watcher started...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
111.         }
112. 
113.         private void RegisterBackgroundTask(DeviceWatcherTrigger deviceWatcherTrigger)
114.         {
115.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
116.             BackgroundTaskBuilder taskBuilder;
117. 
118.             // First see if we already have this background task registered. If so, unregister
119.             // it before we register it with the new trigger.
120. 
121.             foreach (var task in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)
122.             {
123.                 if (backgroundTaskName == task.Value.Name)
124.                 {
125.                     UnregisterBackgroundTask(task.Value);
126.                 }
127.             }
128. 
129.             // TODO[migrate-011]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
130.             taskBuilder = new BackgroundTaskBuilder();
131.             taskBuilder.Name = backgroundTaskName;
132.             taskBuilder.TaskEntryPoint = "BackgroundDeviceWatcherTaskCs.BackgroundDeviceWatcher";
133.             taskBuilder.SetTrigger(deviceWatcherTrigger);
134. 
135.             backgroundTaskRegistration = taskBuilder.Register();
136.             backgroundTaskRegistration.Completed += OnTaskCompleted;
137.         }
138. 
139.         private async void OnTaskCompleted(BackgroundTaskRegistration task, BackgroundTaskCompletedEventArgs args)
140.         {
141.             // TODO[migrate-012]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
142.             await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
143.             {
144.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Background task completed", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
145.             });
146.         }
147. 
148.         private void StopWatcher()
149.         {
150.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
151.             if (null != backgroundTaskRegistration)
152.             {
153.                 UnregisterBackgroundTask(backgroundTaskRegistration);
154.             }
155.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
156.         }
157. 
158.         private void UnregisterBackgroundTask(IBackgroundTaskRegistration taskReg)
159.         {
160.             taskReg.Unregister(true);
161.             ApplicationDataContainer settings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
162.             settings.Values["eventCount"] = (uint)0;
163.         }
164.     }

77.             // can be paired or are already paired.
78.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
79.             string selector = "(" + deviceSelectorInfo.Selector + ")" + " AND (System.Devices.Aep.CanPair:=System.StructuredQueryType.Boolean#True OR System.Devices.Aep.IsPaired:=System.StructuredQueryType.Boolean#True)";

120.         private async void PairButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
121.         {
122.             // Gray out the pair button and results view while pairing is in progress.
123.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = false;
124.             pairButton.IsEnabled = false;
125.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Pairing started. Please wait...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
126. 
127.             // Get the device selected for pairing
128.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = resultsListView.SelectedItem as DeviceInformationDisplay;
129. 
130.             // Get ceremony type and protection level selections
131.             DevicePairingKinds ceremoniesSelected = GetSelectedCeremonies();
132.             ProtectionLevelSelectorInfo protectionLevelInfo = (ProtectionLevelSelectorInfo)protectionLevelComboBox.SelectedItem;
133.             DevicePairingProtectionLevel protectionLevel = protectionLevelInfo.ProtectionLevel;
134. 
135.             DeviceInformationCustomPairing customPairing = deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.Custom;
136. 
137.             customPairing.PairingRequested += PairingRequestedHandler;
138.             DevicePairingResult result = await customPairing.PairAsync(ceremoniesSelected, protectionLevel);
139.             customPairing.PairingRequested -= PairingRequestedHandler;
140. 
141.             rootPage.NotifyUser(
142.                 "Pairing result = " + result.Status.ToString(),
143.                 result.Status == DevicePairingResultStatus.Paired ? NotifyType.StatusMessage : NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
144. 
145.             HidePairingPanel();
146.             UpdatePairingButtons();
147.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = true;

154.             unpairButton.IsEnabled = false;

156.

160.

163.                 dupr.Status == DeviceUnpairingResultStatus.Unpaired ? NotifyType.StatusMessage : NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
164.

166.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = true;

168. 
169.         private async void PairingRequestedHandler(
170.             DeviceInformationCustomPairing sender,
171.             DevicePairingRequestedEventArgs args)
172.         {
173.             switch (args.PairingKind)
174.             {
175.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmOnly:
176.                     // Windows itself will pop the confirmation dialog as part of "consent" if this is running on Desktop or Mobile
177.                     // If this is an App for 'Windows IoT Core' where there is no Windows Consent UX, you may want to provide your own confirmation.
178.                     args.Accept();
179.                     break;
180. 
181.                 case DevicePairingKinds.DisplayPin:
182.                     // We just show the PIN on this side. The ceremony is actually completed when the user enters the PIN
183.                     // on the target device. We automatically accept here since we can't really "cancel" the operation
184.                     // from this side.
185.                     args.Accept();
186. 
187.                     // No need for a deferral since we don't need any decision from the user
188.                     // TODO[migrate-013]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
189.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
190.                     {
191.                         ShowPairingPanel(
192.                             "Please enter this PIN on the device you are pairing with: " + args.Pin,
193.                             args.PairingKind);
194. 
195.                     });
196.                     break;
197. 
198.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePin:
199.                     // A PIN may be shown on the target device and the user needs to enter the matching PIN on
200.                     // this Windows device. Get a deferral so we can perform the async request to the user.
201.                     var collectPinDeferral = args.GetDeferral();
202. 
203.                     // TODO[migrate-014]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
204.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, async () =>
205.                     {
206.                         string pin = await GetPinFromUserAsync();
207.                         if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(pin))
208.                         {
209.                             args.Accept(pin);
210.                         }
211. 
212.                         collectPinDeferral.Complete();
213.                     });
214.                     break;
215. 
216.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePasswordCredential:
217.                     var collectCredentialDeferral = args.GetDeferral();
218.                     // TODO[migrate-015]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
219.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, async () =>
220.                     {
221.                         var credential = await GetPasswordCredentialFromUserAsync();
222.                         if (credential != null)
223.                         {
224.                             args.AcceptWithPasswordCredential(credential);
225.                         }
226.                         collectCredentialDeferral.Complete();
227.                     });
228.                     break;
229. 
230.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmPinMatch:
231.                     // We show the PIN here and the user responds with whether the PIN matches what they see
232.                     // on the target device. Response comes back and we set it on the PinComparePairingRequestedData
233.                     // then complete the deferral.
234.                     var displayMessageDeferral = args.GetDeferral();
235. 
236.                     // TODO[migrate-016]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
237.                     await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, async () =>
238.                     {
239.                         bool accept = await GetUserConfirmationAsync(args.Pin);
240.                         if (accept)
241.                         {
242.                             args.Accept();
243.                         }
244. 
245.                         displayMessageDeferral.Complete();
246.                     });
247.                     break;
248.             }
249.         }
250. 
251.         private void ShowPairingPanel(string text, DevicePairingKinds pairingKind)
252.         {
253.             pairingPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
254.             pinEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
255.             okButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
256.             usernameEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
257.             passwordEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
258.             verifyButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
259.             yesButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
260.             noButton.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
261.             pairingTextBlock.Text = text;
262. 
263.             switch (pairingKind)
264.             {
265.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmOnly:
266.                 case DevicePairingKinds.DisplayPin:
267.                     // Don't need any buttons
268.                     break;
269.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePin:
270.                     pinEntryTextBox.Text = "";
271.                     pinEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
272.                     okButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
273.                     break;
274.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmPinMatch:
275.                     yesButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
276.                     noButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
277.                     break;
278.                 case DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePasswordCredential:
279.                     usernameEntryTextBox.Text = "";
280.                     passwordEntryTextBox.Text = "";
281.                     passwordEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
282.                     usernameEntryTextBox.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
283.                     verifyButton.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
284.                     break;
285.             }
286. 
287.             pairingPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
288.         }
289. 
290.         private void HidePairingPanel()
291.         {
292.             pairingPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
293.             pairingTextBlock.Text = "";
294.         }
295. 
296.         private async Task<string> GetPinFromUserAsync()
297.         {
298.             HidePairingPanel();
299.             CompleteProvidePinTask(); // Abandon any previous pin request.
300. 
301.             ShowPairingPanel(
302.                 "Please enter the PIN shown on the device you're pairing with",
303.                 DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePin);
304. 
305.             providePinTaskSrc = new TaskCompletionSource<string>();
306. 
307.             return await providePinTaskSrc.Task;
308.         }
309. 
310.         // If pin is not provided, then any pending pairing request is abandoned.
311.         private void CompleteProvidePinTask(string pin = null)
312.         {
313.             if (providePinTaskSrc != null)
314.             {
315.                 providePinTaskSrc.SetResult(pin);
316.                 providePinTaskSrc = null;
317.             }
318.         }
319. 
320.         private async Task<PasswordCredential> GetPasswordCredentialFromUserAsync()
321.         {
322.             HidePairingPanel();
323.             CompletePasswordCredential(); // Abandon any previous pin request.
324. 
325.             ShowPairingPanel(
326.                 "Please enter the username and password",
327.                 DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePasswordCredential);
328. 
329.             providePasswordCredential = new TaskCompletionSource<PasswordCredential>();
330. 
331.             return await providePasswordCredential.Task;
332.         }
333. 
334.         private void CompletePasswordCredential(string username = null, string password = null)
335.         {
336.             if (providePasswordCredential != null)
337.             {
338.                 if (String.IsNullOrEmpty(username))
339.                 {
340.                     providePasswordCredential.SetResult(null);
341.                 }
342.                 else
343.                 {
344.                     providePasswordCredential.SetResult(new PasswordCredential() { UserName = username, Password = password });
345.                 }
346.                 providePasswordCredential = null;
347.             }
348.         }
349. 
350.         private async Task<bool> GetUserConfirmationAsync(string pin)
351.         {
352.             HidePairingPanel();
353.             CompleteConfirmPinTask(false); // Abandon any previous request.
354. 
355.             ShowPairingPanel(
356.                 "Does the following PIN match the one shown on the device you are pairing?: " + pin,
357.                 DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmPinMatch);
358. 
359.             confirmPinTaskSrc = new TaskCompletionSource<bool>();
360. 
361.             return await confirmPinTaskSrc.Task;
362.         }
363. 
364.         // If pin is not provided, then any pending pairing request is abandoned.
365.         private void CompleteConfirmPinTask(bool accept)
366.         {
367.             if (confirmPinTaskSrc != null)
368.             {
369.                 confirmPinTaskSrc.SetResult(accept);
370.                 confirmPinTaskSrc = null;
371.             }
372.         }
373. 
374.         private void okButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
375.         {
376.             // OK button is only used for the ProvidePin scenario
377.             CompleteProvidePinTask(pinEntryTextBox.Text);
378.             HidePairingPanel();
379.         }
380. 
381.         private void verifyButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
382.         {
383.             // verify button is only used for the ProvidePin scenario
384.             CompletePasswordCredential(usernameEntryTextBox.Text, passwordEntryTextBox.Text);
385.             HidePairingPanel();
386.         }
387. 
388.         private void yesButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
389.         {
390.             CompleteConfirmPinTask(true);
391.             HidePairingPanel();
392.         }
393. 
394.         private void noButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
395.         {
396.             CompleteConfirmPinTask(false);
397.             HidePairingPanel();
398.         }
399. 
400.         private DevicePairingKinds GetSelectedCeremonies()
401.         {
402.             DevicePairingKinds ceremonySelection = DevicePairingKinds.None;
403.             if (confirmOnlyOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmOnly;
404.             if (displayPinOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.DisplayPin;
405.             if (providePinOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePin;
406.             if (confirmPinMatchOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.ConfirmPinMatch;
407.             if (passwordCredentialOption.IsChecked.Value) ceremonySelection |= DevicePairingKinds.ProvidePasswordCredential;
408.             return ceremonySelection;
409.         }
410. 
411.         private void ResultsListView_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
412.         {
413.             UpdatePairingButtons();
414.         }
415. 
416.         private void UpdatePairingButtons()
417.         {
418.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = (DeviceInformationDisplay)resultsListView.SelectedItem;
419. 
420.             if (null != deviceInfoDisp &&
421.                 deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.CanPair &&
422.                 !deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.IsPaired)
423.             {
424.                 pairButton.IsEnabled = true;
425.             }
426.             else
427.             {
428.                 pairButton.IsEnabled = false;
429.             }
430. 
431.             if (null != deviceInfoDisp &&
432.                 deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.IsPaired)
433.             {
434.                 unpairButton.IsEnabled = true;
435.             }
436.             else
437.             {
438.                 unpairButton.IsEnabled = false;
439.             }
440.         }
441.     }
442. }
443.

1. // Copyright (c) Microsoft Corporation. All rights reserved.
2. 
3. using System;
4. using System.Collections.Generic;
5. using System.Linq;
6. using System.Text;
7. using System.Threading.Tasks;
8. using Windows.Storage;
9. using System.Diagnostics;
10. 
11. // Needed for the DeviceWatcherTrigger
12. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Background;
13. using Windows.Devices.Enumeration;
14. 
15. namespace BackgroundDeviceWatcherTaskCs
16. {
17.     // TODO[migrate-017]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
18.     public sealed class BackgroundDeviceWatcher : IBackgroundTask
19.     {
20.         public void Run(IBackgroundTaskInstance taskInstance)
21.         {
22.             Debug.WriteLine("BackgroundDeviceWatcher.Run called");
23. 
24.             uint eventCount = 0;
25.             ApplicationDataContainer settings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
26.             DeviceWatcherTriggerDetails triggerDetails = (DeviceWatcherTriggerDetails)taskInstance.TriggerDetails;
27. 
28.             Debug.WriteLine(String.Format("Trigger contains {0} events", triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents.Count));
29. 
30.             foreach (DeviceWatcherEvent e in triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents)
31.             {
32.                 switch (e.Kind)
33.                 {
34.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Add:
35.                     Debug.WriteLine("Add: " + e.DeviceInformation.Id);
36.                     break;
37. 
38.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Update:
39.                     Debug.WriteLine("Update: " + e.DeviceInformationUpdate.Id);
40.                     break;
41. 
42.                 case DeviceWatcherEventKind.Remove:
43.                     Debug.WriteLine("Remove: " + e.DeviceInformationUpdate.Id);
44.                     break;
45.                 }
46.             };
47.             
48.             if (null != settings.Values["eventCount"] &&
49.                 settings.Values["eventCount"].GetType() == typeof(uint))
50.             {
51.                 eventCount = (uint)settings.Values["eventCount"];
52.             }
53. 
54.             // Add the number of events for this trigger to the number of events received in the past
55.             eventCount += (uint)triggerDetails.DeviceWatcherEvents.Count;
56. 
57.             Debug.WriteLine(eventCount + " events processed for lifetime of trigger");
58. 
59.             settings.Values["eventCount"] = eventCount;
60.         }
61.     }
62. }
63. 
--- RESULT ---
<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<exited with exit code 0>

<a id="background-tasks"></a>
## Background Tasks`IBackgroundTask` / `BackgroundTaskRegistration` are not the recommended model. Use the WinAppSDK [`BackgroundTaskBuilder`](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/windows-app-sdk/api/winrt/microsoft.windows.applicationmodel.background.backgroundtaskbuilder) (introduced in 1.7), or move the work to push-driven activation / Windows Task Scheduler. See the [background task migration strategy](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/background-task-migration-strategy).

281.         private async void UpdateGlyphBitmapImage()
282.         {
283.             DeviceThumbnail deviceThumbnail = await DeviceInformation.GetGlyphThumbnailAsync();

287.             OnPropertyChanged("GlyphBitmapImage");
288.         }

22. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402352&clcid=0x409

24. namespace SDKTemplate
25. {
26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }

152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)

17.     /// <summary>
18.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
19.     /// </summary>
20.     public sealed partial class Scenario7_DeviceInformationKind : Page
21.     {
22.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
23. 
24.         private List<DeviceWatcherHelper> deviceWatcherHelpers = new List<DeviceWatcherHelper>();
25. 
26.         private ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection = new ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay>();
27. 
28.         public Scenario7_DeviceInformationKind()
29.         {
30.             this.InitializeComponent();
31.         }
32. 
33.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
34.         {
35.             resultsListView.ItemsSource = resultCollection;
36. 
37.             kindComboBox.ItemsSource = DeviceInformationKindChoices.Choices;
38.             kindComboBox.SelectedIndex = 0;
39.         }
40. 
41.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
42.         {
43.             StopWatchers(/* reset */ true);
44.         }
45. 
46.         private void StartWatcherButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
47.         {
48.             StartWatchers();
49.         }
50. 
51.         private void StopWatcherButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
52.         {
53.             StopWatchers();
54.         }
55. 
56.         private void StartWatchers()
57.         {
58.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
59.             resultCollection.Clear();
60. 
61.             // Create a watcher for each DeviceInformationKind selected by the user
62.             foreach (DeviceInformationKind deviceInfoKind in ((DeviceInformationKindChoice)kindComboBox.SelectedItem).DeviceInformationKinds)
63.             {
64.                 DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
65.                         "", // AQS Filter string
66.                         null, // requested properties
67.                         deviceInfoKind);
68. 
69.                 DeviceWatcherHelper deviceWatcherHelper = new DeviceWatcherHelper(resultCollection, Dispatcher);
70.                 deviceWatcherHelper.UpdateStatus = false; // we will show our own status messages
71.                 deviceWatcherHelper.DeviceChanged += OnDeviceListChanged;
72.                 deviceWatcherHelpers.Add(deviceWatcherHelper);
73. 
74.                 deviceWatcherHelper.StartWatcher(deviceWatcher);
75.             }
76. 
77.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
78.             stopWatcherButton.Focus(FocusState.Keyboard);
79.         }
80. 
81.         private void StopWatchers(bool reset = false)
82.         {
83.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
84. 
85.             foreach (DeviceWatcherHelper deviceWatcherHelper in deviceWatcherHelpers)
86.             {
87.                 deviceWatcherHelper.StopWatcher();
88.                 if (reset)
89.                 {
90.                     deviceWatcherHelper.Reset();
91.                 }
92.             }
93.             deviceWatcherHelpers.Clear();
94. 
95.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
96.             startWatcherButton.Focus(FocusState.Keyboard);
97.         }
98. 
99.         private void OnDeviceListChanged(DeviceWatcher sender, string id)
100.         {
101.             int watchersRunning = 0;
102. 
103.             // Count running watchers
104.             foreach (DeviceWatcherHelper deviceWatcherHelper in deviceWatcherHelpers)
105.             {
106.                 if (deviceWatcherHelper.IsWatcherRunning())
107.                 {
108.                     watchersRunning++;
109.                 }
110.             }
111. 
112.             string message = $"{watchersRunning}/{deviceWatcherHelpers.Count} watchers running. {resultCollection.Count} devices found.";
113.             rootPage.NotifyUser(message, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
114.         }

MainPage.xaml.cs - FULL REPLACEMENT\n```csharp\n// Copyright (c) Microsoft. All rights reserved.\n\nusing System;\nusing System.Collections.Generic;\nusing Microsoft.UI;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Automation.Peers;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Controls;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Media;\nusing Microsoft.UI.Xaml.Navigation;\n\nnamespace DeviceEnumerationAndPairing\n{\n    public sealed partial class MainPage : Page\n    {\n        public static MainPage Current;\n\n        public MainPage()\n        {\n            this.InitializeComponent();\n\n            Current = this;\n            SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;\n        }\n\n        protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)\n        {\n            var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();\n            int i = 1;\n            foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)\n            {\n                itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $\"{i++}) {s.Title}\", ClassType = s.ClassType });\n            }\n            ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;\n            ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;\n        }\n\n        private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);\n\n            ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;\n            Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;\n            if (s != null)\n            {\n                ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);\n            }\n        }\n\n        public List<Scenario> Scenarios\n        {\n            get { return this.scenarios; }\n        }\n\n        public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)\n        {\n            if (DispatcherQueue.HasThreadAccess)\n            {\n                UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));\n            }\n        }\n\n        private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)\n        {\n            switch (type)\n            {\n                case NotifyType.StatusMessage:\n                    StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Green);\n                    break;\n                case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:\n                    StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Colors.Red);\n                    break;\n            }\n\n            StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;\n\n            if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)\n            {\n                StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;\n                StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;\n            }\n            else\n            {\n                StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;\n                StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;\n            }\n\n            var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);\n            if (peer != null)\n            {\n                peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);\n            }\n        }\n\n        private void HamburgerButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)\n        {\n            Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;\n        }\n    }\n\n    public enum NotifyType\n    {\n        StatusMessage,\n        ErrorMessage\n    };\n}\n```\n\n### 3. 3. using System;
4. using System.Collections.ObjectModel;
5. using Windows.Devices.Enumeration;
6. using Windows.Foundation;
7. using Windows.UI.Core;
8. 
9. namespace SDKTemplate
10. {
11.     /// <summary>
12.     /// Updates an ObservableCollection based on events from a DeviceWatcher.
13.     /// </summary>
14.     /// <remarks>
15.     /// Encapsulates the work necessary to register for watcher events,
16.     /// start and stop the watcher, handle race conditions, and break cycles.
17.     /// </remarks>
18.     class DeviceWatcherHelper
19.     {
20.         public DeviceWatcherHelper(
21.             ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection,
22.             // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
23.             CoreDispatcher dispatcher)
24.         {
25.             this.resultCollection = resultCollection;
26.             this.dispatcher = dispatcher;
27.         }
28. 
29.         public delegate void DeviceChangedHandler(DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher, string id);
30.         public event DeviceChangedHandler DeviceChanged;
31. 
32.         public DeviceWatcher DeviceWatcher => deviceWatcher;
33.         public bool UpdateStatus = true;
34. 
35.         public void StartWatcher(DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher)
36.         {
37.             this.deviceWatcher = deviceWatcher;
38. 
39.             // Connect events to update our collection as the watcher report results.
40.             deviceWatcher.Added += Watcher_DeviceAdded;
41.             deviceWatcher.Updated += Watcher_DeviceUpdated;
42.             deviceWatcher.Removed += Watcher_DeviceRemoved;
43.             deviceWatcher.EnumerationCompleted += Watcher_EnumerationCompleted;
44.             deviceWatcher.Stopped += Watcher_Stopped;
45. 
46.             deviceWatcher.Start();
47.         }
48. 
49.         public void StopWatcher()
50.         {
51.             // Since the device watcher runs in the background, it is possible that
52.             // a notification is "in flight" at the time we stop the watcher.
53.             // In other words, it is possible for the watcher to become stopped while a
54.             // handler is running, or for a handler to run after the watcher has stopped.
55. 
56.             if (IsWatcherStarted(deviceWatcher))
57.             {
58.                 // We do not null out the deviceWatcher yet because we want to receive
59.                 // the Stopped event.
60.                 deviceWatcher.Stop();
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         public void Reset()
65.         {
66.             if (deviceWatcher != null)
67.             {
68.                 StopWatcher();
69.                 deviceWatcher = null;
70.             }
71.         }
72. 
73.         DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = null;
74.         ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection;
75.         // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
76.         CoreDispatcher dispatcher;
77. 
78.         static bool IsWatcherStarted(DeviceWatcher watcher)
79.         {
80.             return (watcher.Status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Started) ||
81.                 (watcher.Status == DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted);
82.         }
83. 
84.         public bool IsWatcherRunning()
85.         {
86.             if (deviceWatcher == null)
87.             {
88.                 return false;
89.             }
90. 
91.             DeviceWatcherStatus status = deviceWatcher.Status;
92.             return (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Started) ||
93.                 (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted) ||
94.                 (status == DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopping);
95.         }
96. 
97.         private async void Watcher_DeviceAdded(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformation deviceInfo)
98.         {
99.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
100.             // TODO[migrate-003]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
101.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
102.             {
103.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
104.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
105.                 {
106.                     resultCollection.Add(new DeviceInformationDisplay(deviceInfo));
107.                     RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfo.Id);
108.                 }
109.             });
110.         }
111. 
112.         private async void Watcher_DeviceUpdated(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)
113.         {
114.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
115.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
116.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
117.             {
118.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
119.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
120.                 {
121.                     // Find the corresponding updated DeviceInformation in the collection and pass the update object
122.                     // to the Update method of the existing DeviceInformation. This automatically updates the object
123.                     // for us.
124.                     foreach (DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp in resultCollection)
125.                     {
126.                         if (deviceInfoDisp.Id == deviceInfoUpdate.Id)
127.                         {
128.                             deviceInfoDisp.Update(deviceInfoUpdate);
129.                             RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfoUpdate.Id);
130.                             break;
131.                         }
132.                     }
133.                 }
134.             });
135.         }
136. 
137.         private async void Watcher_DeviceRemoved(DeviceWatcher sender, DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)
138.         {
139.             // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
140.             // TODO[migrate-005]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
141.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
142.             {
143.                 // Watcher may have stopped while we were waiting for our chance to run.
144.                 if (IsWatcherStarted(sender))
145.                 {
146.                     // Find the corresponding DeviceInformation in the collection and remove it
147.                     foreach (DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp in resultCollection)
148.                     {
149.                         if (deviceInfoDisp.Id == deviceInfoUpdate.Id)
150.                         {
151.                             resultCollection.Remove(deviceInfoDisp);
152.                             break;
153.                         }
154.                     }
155. 
156.                     RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, deviceInfoUpdate.Id);
157.                 }
158.             });
159.         }
160. 
161.         private async void Watcher_EnumerationCompleted(DeviceWatcher sender, object obj)
162.         {
163.             // TODO[migrate-006]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
164.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
165.             {
166.                 RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, string.Empty);
167.             });
168.         }
169. 
170.         private async void Watcher_Stopped(DeviceWatcher sender, object obj)
171.         {
172.             // TODO[migrate-007]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
173.             await dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
174.             {
175.                 RaiseDeviceChanged(sender, string.Empty);
176.             });
177.         }
178. 
179.         private void RaiseDeviceChanged(DeviceWatcher sender, string id)
180.         {
181.             if (UpdateStatus)
182.             {
183.                 // Display a default status message.
184.                 string message = string.Empty;
185.                 switch (sender.Status)
186.                 {
187.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Started:
188.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found.";
189.                         break;
190. 
191.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.EnumerationCompleted:
192.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Enumeration completed. Watching for updates...";
193.                         break;
194. 
195.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Stopped:
196.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Watcher stopped.";
197.                         break;
198. 
199.                     case DeviceWatcherStatus.Aborted:
200.                         message = $"{resultCollection.Count} devices found. Watcher aborted.";
201.                         break;
202.                 }
203. 
204.                 if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(message))
205.                 {
206.                     MainPage.Current.NotifyUser(message, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
207.                 }
208.             }
209. 
210.             DeviceChanged?.Invoke(sender, id);
211.         }
212.     }
213. }

247.     public class DeviceInformationDisplay : INotifyPropertyChanged
248.     {
249.         public DeviceInformationDisplay(DeviceInformation deviceInfoIn)
250.         {
251.             DeviceInformation = deviceInfoIn;
252.             UpdateGlyphBitmapImage();
253.         }
254. 
255.         public DeviceInformationKind Kind => DeviceInformation.Kind;
256.         public string Id => DeviceInformation.Id;
257.         public string Name => DeviceInformation.Name;
258.         public BitmapImage GlyphBitmapImage { get; private set; }
259.         public bool CanPair => DeviceInformation.Pairing.CanPair;
260.         public bool IsPaired => DeviceInformation.Pairing.IsPaired;
261.         public IReadOnlyDictionary<string, object> Properties => DeviceInformation.Properties;
262.         public DeviceInformation DeviceInformation { get; private set; }
263. 
264.         public void Update(DeviceInformationUpdate deviceInfoUpdate)
265.         {
266.             DeviceInformation.Update(deviceInfoUpdate);
267. 
268.             OnPropertyChanged("Kind");
269.             OnPropertyChanged("Id");
270.             OnPropertyChanged("Name");
271.             OnPropertyChanged("DeviceInformation");
272.             OnPropertyChanged("CanPair");
273.             OnPropertyChanged("IsPaired");
274.             OnPropertyChanged("GetPropertyForDisplay");
275. 
276.             UpdateGlyphBitmapImage();
277.         }
278. 
279.         public string GetPropertyForDisplay(string key) => Properties[key]?.ToString();
280. 
281.         private async void UpdateGlyphBitmapImage()
282.         {
283.             DeviceThumbnail deviceThumbnail = await DeviceInformation.GetGlyphThumbnailAsync();
284.             BitmapImage glyphBitmapImage = new BitmapImage();
285.             await glyphBitmapImage.SetSourceAsync(deviceThumbnail);
286.             GlyphBitmapImage = glyphBitmapImage;
287.             OnPropertyChanged("GlyphBitmapImage");
288.         }
289. 
290.         public event PropertyChangedEventHandler PropertyChanged;
291.         protected void OnPropertyChanged(string name) => PropertyChanged?.Invoke(this, new PropertyChangedEventArgs(name));
292.     }

24.         private ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay> resultCollection = new ObservableCollection<DeviceInformationDisplay>();

26.         public Scenario1_DevicePicker()
27.         {
28.             this.InitializeComponent();
29.         }
30. 
31.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
32.         {
33.             resultsListView.ItemsSource = resultCollection;
34.             selectorComboBox.ItemsSource = DeviceSelectorChoices.DevicePickerSelectors;
35.             selectorComboBox.SelectedIndex = 0;
36.         }

38.         private void PickSingleDeviceButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
39.         {
40.             ShowDevicePicker(
41.                 true // pickSingle
42.                 );
43.         }

46.         {
47.             ShowDevicePicker(
48.                 false // pickSingle
49.                 );
50.         }
51. 
52.         private async void ShowDevicePicker(bool pickSingle)
53.         {
54.             showDevicePickerButton.IsEnabled = false;
55.             resultCollection.Clear();
56. 
57.             var devicePicker = new DevicePicker();
58. 
59.             // First get the device selector chosen by the UI.
60.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
61. 
62.             if (null == deviceSelectorInfo.Selector)
63.             {
64.                 // If the a pre-canned device class selector was chosen, call the DeviceClass overload
65.                 devicePicker.Filter.SupportedDeviceClasses.Add(deviceSelectorInfo.DeviceClassSelector);
66.             }
67.             else
68.             {
69.                 // Use AQS string selector from dynamic call to a device api's GetDeviceSelector call
70.                 devicePicker.Filter.SupportedDeviceSelectors.Add(deviceSelectorInfo.Selector);
71.             }
72. 
73.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Showing Device Picker", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             // Calculate the position to show the picker (right below the buttons)
76.             GeneralTransform ge = pickSingleDeviceButton.TransformToVisual(null);
77.             Point point = ge.TransformPoint(new Point());
78.             Rect rect = new Rect(point, new Point(point.X + pickSingleDeviceButton.ActualWidth, point.Y + pickSingleDeviceButton.ActualHeight));
79. 
80.             if (pickSingle)
81.             {
82.                 DeviceInformation di = await devicePicker.PickSingleDeviceAsync(rect);
83.                 if (null != di)
84.                 {
85.                     resultCollection.Add(new DeviceInformationDisplay(di));
86.                 }
87.                 showDevicePickerButton.IsEnabled = true;
88.             }
89.             else
90.             {
91.                 devicePicker.DevicePickerDismissed += new TypedEventHandler<DevicePicker, object>(
92.                 async (picker, obj) =>
93.                 {
94.                     // Must access UI elements from the UI thread.
95.                     // TODO[migrate-008]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
96.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
97.                     {
98.                         showDevicePickerButton.IsEnabled = true;
99.                         rootPage.NotifyUser("Hiding Device Picker", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
100.                     });
101.                 });
102. 
103.                 devicePicker.DeviceSelected += new TypedEventHandler<DevicePicker, DeviceSelectedEventArgs>(
104.                 async (picker, args) =>
105.                 {
106.                     // Since we have the collection databound to a UI element, we need to update the collection on the UI thread.
107.                     // TODO[migrate-009]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
108.                     await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Low, () =>
109.                     {
110.                         resultCollection.Clear();
111.                         resultCollection.Add(new DeviceInformationDisplay(args.SelectedDevice));
112.                     });
113.                 });
114. 
115.                 // Show the picker
116.                 devicePicker.Show(rect);
117.             }
118.         }
119.     }

3. using System;
4. using Windows.ApplicationModel.Background;
5. using Windows.Devices.Enumeration;
6. using Windows.Storage;

15. {
16.     /// <summary>
17.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
18.     /// </summary>
19.     public sealed partial class Scenario3_BackgroundDeviceWatcher : Page
20.     {
21.         private MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
22. 
23.         private string backgroundTaskName = "DeviceEnumerationCs_BackgroundTaskName";
24.         private IBackgroundTaskRegistration backgroundTaskRegistration = null;
25. 
26.         public Scenario3_BackgroundDeviceWatcher()
27.         {
28.             this.InitializeComponent();
29.         }
30. 
31.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
32.         {
33.             selectorComboBox.ItemsSource = DeviceSelectorChoices.BackgroundDeviceWatcherSelectors;
34.             selectorComboBox.SelectedIndex = 0;
35. 
36.             // Determine if the background task is already active
37.             foreach (var task in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)
38.             {
39.                 if (backgroundTaskName == task.Value.Name)
40.                 {
41.                     backgroundTaskRegistration = task.Value;
42.                     startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
43.                     stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
44.                     break;
45.                 }
46.             }
47.         }
48. 
49.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
50.         {
51.             if (backgroundTaskRegistration != null)
52.             {
53.                 backgroundTaskRegistration.Completed -= OnTaskCompleted;
54.                 backgroundTaskRegistration = null;
55.             }
56.         }
57. 
58.         private void StartWatcherButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
59.         {
60.             StartWatcher();
61.         }
62. 
63.         private void StopWatcherButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
64.         {
65.             StopWatcher();
66.         }
67. 
68.         private void StartWatcher()
69.         {
70.             DeviceWatcherEventKind[] triggerEventKinds = { DeviceWatcherEventKind.Add, DeviceWatcherEventKind.Remove, DeviceWatcherEventKind.Update };
71.             DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher = null;
72. 
73.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
74. 
75.             // First get the device selector chosen by the UI.
76.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
77. 
78.             if (null == deviceSelectorInfo.Selector)
79.             {
80.                 // If the a pre-canned device class selector was chosen, call the DeviceClass overload
81.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(deviceSelectorInfo.DeviceClassSelector);
82.             }
83.             else if (deviceSelectorInfo.Kind == DeviceInformationKind.Unknown)
84.             {
85.                 // Use AQS string selector from dynamic call to a device api's GetDeviceSelector call
86.                 // Kind will be determined by the selector
87.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
88.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Selector,
89.                     null // don't request additional properties for this sample
90.                     );
91.             }
92.             else
93.             {
94.                 // Kind is specified in the selector info
95.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
96.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Selector,
97.                     null, // don't request additional properties for this sample
98.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Kind);
99.             }
100. 
101.             // Get the background trigger for this watcher
102.             DeviceWatcherTrigger deviceWatcherTrigger = deviceWatcher.GetBackgroundTrigger(triggerEventKinds);
103. 
104.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Starting Watcher...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
105. 
106.             // Register this trigger for our background task
107.             RegisterBackgroundTask(deviceWatcherTrigger);
108. 
109.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
110.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Watcher started...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
111.         }
112. 
113.         private void RegisterBackgroundTask(DeviceWatcherTrigger deviceWatcherTrigger)
114.         {
115.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
116.             BackgroundTaskBuilder taskBuilder;
117. 
118.             // First see if we already have this background task registered. If so, unregister
119.             // it before we register it with the new trigger.
120. 
121.             foreach (var task in BackgroundTaskRegistration.AllTasks)
122.             {
123.                 if (backgroundTaskName == task.Value.Name)
124.                 {
125.                     UnregisterBackgroundTask(task.Value);
126.                 }
127.             }
128. 
129.             // TODO[migrate-011]: see PATTERNS.md#background-tasks
130.             taskBuilder = new BackgroundTaskBuilder();
131.             taskBuilder.Name = backgroundTaskName;
132.             taskBuilder.TaskEntryPoint = "BackgroundDeviceWatcherTaskCs.BackgroundDeviceWatcher";
133.             taskBuilder.SetTrigger(deviceWatcherTrigger);
134. 
135.             backgroundTaskRegistration = taskBuilder.Register();
136.             backgroundTaskRegistration.Completed += OnTaskCompleted;
137.         }
138. 
139.         private async void OnTaskCompleted(BackgroundTaskRegistration task, BackgroundTaskCompletedEventArgs args)
140.         {
141.             // TODO[migrate-012]: see PATTERNS.md#threading
142.             await rootPage.Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () =>
143.             {
144.                 rootPage.NotifyUser("Background task completed", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
145.             });
146.         }
147. 
148.         private void StopWatcher()
149.         {
150.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
151.             if (null != backgroundTaskRegistration)
152.             {
153.                 UnregisterBackgroundTask(backgroundTaskRegistration);
154.             }
155.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
156.         }
157. 
158.         private void UnregisterBackgroundTask(IBackgroundTaskRegistration taskReg)
159.         {
160.             taskReg.Unregister(true);
161.             ApplicationDataContainer settings = ApplicationData.Current.LocalSettings;
162.             settings.Values["eventCount"] = (uint)0;
163.         }
164.     }
165. }

70.         private void StartWatcher()
71.         {
72.             startWatcherButton.IsEnabled = false;
73.             resultCollection.Clear();
74.             DeviceWatcher deviceWatcher;
75. 
76.             // Get the device selector chosen by the UI then add additional constraints for devices that
77.             // can be paired or are already paired.
78.             DeviceSelectorInfo deviceSelectorInfo = (DeviceSelectorInfo)selectorComboBox.SelectedItem;
79.             string selector = "(" + deviceSelectorInfo.Selector + ")" + " AND (System.Devices.Aep.CanPair:=System.StructuredQueryType.Boolean#True OR System.Devices.Aep.IsPaired:=System.StructuredQueryType.Boolean#True)";
80. 
81.             if (deviceSelectorInfo.Kind == DeviceInformationKind.Unknown)
82.             {
83.                 // Kind will be determined by the selector
84.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
85.                     selector,
86.                     null // don't request additional properties for this sample
87.                     );
88.             }
89.             else
90.             {
91.                 // Kind is specified in the selector info
92.                 deviceWatcher = DeviceInformation.CreateWatcher(
93.                     selector,
94.                     null, // don't request additional properties for this sample
95.                     deviceSelectorInfo.Kind);
96.             }
97. 
98.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Starting Watcher...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
99.             deviceWatcherHelper.StartWatcher(deviceWatcher);
100.             stopWatcherButton.IsEnabled = true;
101.         }

110.         private void OnDeviceListChanged(DeviceWatcher sender, string id)

113.             DeviceInformationDisplay selectedDeviceInfoDisp = (DeviceInformationDisplay)resultsListView.SelectedItem;

120.         private async void PairButton_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
121.         {
122.             // Gray out the pair button and results view while pairing is in progress.
123.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = false;
124.             pairButton.IsEnabled = false;
125.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Pairing started. Please wait...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
126. 
127.             // Get the device selected for pairing
128.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = resultsListView.SelectedItem as DeviceInformationDisplay;
129. 
130.             // Get ceremony type and protection level selections
131.             DevicePairingKinds ceremoniesSelected = GetSelectedCeremonies();
132.             ProtectionLevelSelectorInfo protectionLevelInfo = (ProtectionLevelSelectorInfo)protectionLevelComboBox.SelectedItem;
133.             DevicePairingProtectionLevel protectionLevel = protectionLevelInfo.ProtectionLevel;
134. 
135.             DeviceInformationCustomPairing customPairing = deviceInfoDisp.DeviceInformation.Pairing.Custom;
136. 
137.             customPairing.PairingRequested += PairingRequestedHandler;
138.             DevicePairingResult result = await customPairing.PairAsync(ceremoniesSelected, protectionLevel);
139.             customPairing.PairingRequested -= PairingRequestedHandler;
140. 
141.             rootPage.NotifyUser(
142.                 "Pairing result = " + result.Status.ToString(),
143.                 result.Status == DevicePairingResultStatus.Paired ? NotifyType.StatusMessage : NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
144. 
145.             HidePairingPanel();
146.             UpdatePairingButtons();
147.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = true;
148.         }

151.         {

153.             resultsListView.IsEnabled = false;
154.             unpairButton.IsEnabled = false;
155.             rootPage.NotifyUser("Unpairing started. Please wait...", NotifyType.StatusMessage);
156. 
157.             DeviceInformationDisplay deviceInfoDisp = resultsListView.SelectedItem as DeviceInformationDisplay;

160.

163.                 dupr.Status == DeviceUnpairingResultStatus.Unpaired ? NotifyType.StatusMessage : NotifyType.ErrorMessage);
164.
